Using the relations in eq. (
A.12
), in terms of the basis used for the structure functions in the body of the paper, we have

\displaystyle(W\cdot L)_{UU}

\displaystyle=\frac{\epsilon-1}{2x}F_{L}^{D}+\frac{1}{2x}F_{2}^{D}+\frac{\epsilon\cos(2\phi)}{2x}F_{3}^{D}-\frac{\sqrt{2\epsilon(1+\epsilon)}}{2x}\cos\phi F_{4}^{D}\,,

(A.25)

which agrees with eq. (
2.60
) in the massless limit up to the overall
2Q^{2}/(1-\epsilon)
that was factored out in eq. (
A.18
). Note that using eqs. (
2.2
) and (
A.25
), we express
\cos\phi
in terms of the Lorentz invariants from
section
2.1
in eq. (
2.61
). By computing the coefficients of polarized scattering involving
\sin\phi
in the Lorentz-invariant form, we can derive the Lorentz-invariant form of
\sin\phi
as

\displaystyle\sin\phi=\frac{1}{Q^{2}}\frac{y}{\sqrt{1-y}}\epsilon^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}k_{\mu}U_{\nu}X_{\rho}q_{\sigma}=-\frac{y}{\sqrt{1-y}}\frac{k\cdot\tilde{n}_{y}}{Q}\,.

(A.26)

As a cross-check, eqs. (
2.61
) and (
A.26
) satisfy
\cos^{2}\phi+\sin^{2}\phi=1
.

In
appendix
A.1.1
, we decompose the hadronic tensor into 18 independent structure functions using orthogonalized four-vectors
\{U,X,\bar{U},\bar{X}\}
. As explained in
section
2.2
, ten of these are leading power (four unpolarized and six polarized), which arise in combinations

\displaystyle F_{i}^{D}+\tilde{S}_{T}^{\prime}F_{iP}^{D}\,,

(A.27)

for
i=\{L,2,3,4,4A\}
and with
\tilde{S}_{T}^{\prime}
from eq. (
2.56
).
Just as above, we can derive the relationship between
\{F_{4A}^{D},F_{iP}^{D}\}
and

W_{i}^{X}
. Four of these leading polarized pieces come from
(W\cdot L)_{UT}
of eq. (
A.22
) as

\displaystyle S_{T}\,(W\cdot L)_{UT}=

\displaystyle S_{T}\biggl\{\sin\left(\phi\!-\!\phi_{S}\right)\left[W_{-1}^{T_{y}}+\epsilon W_{0}^{T_{y}}\right]+\frac{\epsilon}{2}\left[\sin\left(3\phi\!-\!\phi_{S}\right)W_{3}^{T_{y}}-\sin\left(\phi\!+\!\phi_{S}\right)W_{3}^{T_{y}}\right]

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{2\epsilon(1+\epsilon)}\left[\sin\left(2\phi-\phi_{S}\right)W_{1}^{T_{y}}-\sin\left(\phi_{S}\right)W_{1}^{T_{y}}\right]\biggr\}+\cdots{}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle S_{T}\sin\left(\phi\!-\!\phi_{S}\right)\Big\{W_{-1}^{T_{y}}+\epsilon W_{0}^{T_{y}}+\sqrt{2\epsilon(1+\epsilon)}\cos(\phi)W_{1}^{T_{y}}+\epsilon\cos(2\phi)W_{3}^{T_{y}}\Big\}{}

\displaystyle+\cdots\,,

(A.28)

where ellipses indicate subleading terms. Comparing the final line of eq. (
A.1.2
) with the unpolarized case in eq. (
A.24
), we indeed find a parallel structure, grouping each term into

\displaystyle(W\cdot L)_{UU}+S_{T}\,(W\cdot L)_{UT}=

\displaystyle\left(W_{-1}^{U}+S_{T}\sin\left(\phi\!-\!\phi_{S}\right)W_{-1}^{T_{y}}\right)+\epsilon\left(W_{0}^{U}+S_{T}\sin\left(\phi\!-\!\phi_{S}\right)W_{0}^{T_{y}}\right){}

\displaystyle+\sqrt{2\epsilon(1+\epsilon)}\cos(\phi)\left(W_{1}^{U}+S_{T}\sin\left(\phi\!-\!\phi_{S}\right)W_{1}^{T_{y}}\right){}

\displaystyle+\epsilon\cos(2\phi)\left(W_{3}^{U}+S_{T}\sin\left(\phi\!-\!\phi_{S}\right)W_{3}^{T_{y}}\right)+\ldots\,,

(A.29)

reminiscent of eq. (
A.27
). Indeed, the overall
S_{T}\sin\left(\phi-\phi_{S}\right)

in eq. (
A.17
) is identical to
\tilde{S}^{\prime}_{T}
in eq. (
2.56
),

\displaystyle S_{T}\sin(\phi-\phi_{S})

\displaystyle=-S_{\mu}\cdot n_{y,\rm{BR}}^{\mu}=\frac{2x}{|\vec{p}\,^{\prime}_{\perp}|Q^{2}\sqrt{1+\gamma^{2}}}S_{\mu}p_{\nu}p\,^{\prime}_{\rho}q_{\sigma}\epsilon^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}=\tilde{S}^{\prime}_{T}\,,

(A.30)

and leads to an extension of eq. (
A.12
) to the leading polarized structure functions

\displaystyle F_{LP}^{D}=2x\,W_{0}^{T_{y}}\,,

\displaystyle F_{2P}^{D}=2x\,(W_{0}^{T_{y}}+W_{-1}^{T_{y}})\,,

\displaystyle F_{3P}^{D}=2x\,W_{3}^{T_{y}}\,,

\displaystyle F_{4P}^{D}=-2x\,W_{1}^{T_{y}}\,.

(A.31)

Two additional leading structures arise from
(W\cdot L)_{LU}
and
(W\cdot L)_{LT}
, which combine naturally as

\displaystyle\lambda_{\ell}(W\cdot L)_{LU}+\lambda_{\ell}S_{T}\,(W\cdot L)_{LT}=

\displaystyle\lambda_{\ell}\,\sqrt{2\epsilon(1-\epsilon)}\sin\left(\phi\right)\left(W_{2}^{U}+S_{T}\sin\left(\phi\!-\!\phi_{S}\right)W_{2}^{T_{y}}\right)+\cdots\,,

(A.32)

where ellipses again indicate subleading terms. Comparing the decomposition eq. (
A.10
) with eq. (
2.43
), we find that

\displaystyle F_{4A}^{D}=2x\,W_{2}^{U}\,,\qquad F_{4AP}^{D}=2x\,W_{2}^{T_{y}}\,.

(A.33)

The orthogonal vector construction in the main text is useful for constructing independent structure functions in a frame-independent way and to identify the leading power structures. The helicity decomposition here offers a physical interpretation of these structure functions.

A.1.3
Lab and
e
-
p
CM frames

Let us define the electron-proton center-of-mass and lab frames, which are related by a simple boost along the beam direction.
We let
k
and
p
carry momenta in the
\bar{n}
- and
n
-directions defined in
section
2.1.2
respectively, where neglecting the proton mass, we have

\displaystyle k^{\mu}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\gamma}\frac{\sqrt{s}}{2}\,\bar{n}^{\mu}=\frac{1}{\gamma}{\sqrt{s}}\,(1,0,0_{\perp})\,,

\displaystyle p^{\mu}

\displaystyle=\gamma\frac{\sqrt{s}}{2}\,n^{\mu}=\gamma{\sqrt{s}}\,(0,1,0_{\perp})\,.

(A.34)

Here,
s=4E_{p}E_{e}
and the boost factor
\gamma=\sqrt{E_{p}/E_{e}}
allows us to simultaneously write formulas for both the
e^{-}
-
p
CM frame (
\gamma=1
) and the lab frame (
\gamma=\sqrt{E_{p}/E_{e}}\neq 1
).
Using eq. (
2.8
), it is straightforward to express the components of the remaining momenta in
figure
2
in terms of the Lorentz invariants in eqs. (
2.1
) and (
2.1
):

\displaystyle q

\displaystyle=\sqrt{s}\Big(\frac{y}{\gamma},\,-\gamma xy,\,\sqrt{xy(1-y)}\,\Big)

\displaystyle p_{X}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{s}\Bigg(\frac{y(1-z)}{\gamma},\,\gamma(\bar{x}-xy),\,\sqrt{y(\bar{x}-xy)(1-z)-xy\frac{1-\beta}{\beta}-\frac{t}{s}}\,\Bigg)

\displaystyle\tau

\displaystyle=\sqrt{s}\Big(-\!\frac{yz}{\gamma},\,\gamma\bar{x},\,\sqrt{-\bar{x}yz-t/s}\,\Big)

\displaystyle p^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{s}\Big(\frac{yz}{\gamma},\,\gamma(1-\bar{x}),\,\sqrt{-\bar{x}yz-t/s}\,\Big)\,.

(A.35)

Note that the final component of a lightcone coordinate is a magnitude
|\vec{p}_{\perp}|
, and does not represent the full perpendicular vector component of a momentum. Hence, the final components of
q
and
\tau
do not directly sum to the final component of
p_{X}
because in general,
|q_{\perp}|+|\tau_{\perp}|\neq|p_{X\perp}|
. Note that the bounds in
section
2.1
imply that the factors in square roots here are all positive, and that
0<xy<\bar{x}<1
.

Measurement of
\bar{x}
in the lab frame.

In the lab frame, experimental measurements of
\bar{x}
are achieved through measurements of
-t
and the polar angle
\theta
of the recoiling forward system
Y
relative to the incoming proton direction. Writing
p^{\prime}=(E^{\prime},\vec{p}_{\perp}^{\,\prime},p_{z}^{\prime})
and
p_{z}^{\prime}=\kappa E^{\prime}\cos\theta
, where
\kappa=\sqrt{1-m_{Y}^{2}/E^{\prime 2}}
, we find

\displaystyle 1-\bar{x}=\frac{(m_{Y}^{2}-t)}{\gamma^{2}s}\frac{(1+\kappa\cos\theta)}{(1-\kappa\cos\theta)}\,,

(A.36)

and
E^{\prime}=\big[(1-\bar{x})+(m_{Y}^{2}-t)/(\gamma^{2}s)\big]E_{p}
. In the limits
\lambda\ll 1
and
\lambda\lambda_{t}\ll 1
relevant for diffraction, we can expand in
\bar{x},m_{Y}^{2}/s,-t/s\ll 1
, giving the formula quoted in
section
2.4
:

\displaystyle 1-\bar{x}=\frac{m_{Y}^{2}-t}{\gamma^{2}s\tan^{2}(\theta/2)+m_{Y}^{2}}\,.

(A.37)

Interestingly, in the lab frame,
\bar{x}=\tau^{-}/p^{-}
carries the physical interpretation of a longitudinal momentum fraction for the struck parton(s) inside the proton.

Lorentz transformation between lab and Breit frames.

Next, we verify the kinematic relationship between momenta in the Breit and lab frames, as discussed in
section
3.3
.
We begin by recalling the parametrization of the lab frame momentum of a massless particle
r^{\mu}=(r_{t},r_{x},r_{y},r_{z})
in terms of its transverse momentum magnitude
r_{T}
, a transverse momentum angle
\phi
, and (pseudo)rapidity
\mathcal{Y}_{\rm Lab}=\frac{1}{2}\ln(r^{-}/r^{+})=\eta_{\rm Lab}
:

\displaystyle r^{\mu}=r_{T}\bigl(\cosh\eta_{\rm Lab}\,,\cos\phi\,,\sin\phi\,,\sinh\eta_{\rm Lab}\>\bigr)_{\rm Lab}\,.

(A.38)

In lightcone coordinates
(r^{+},r^{-},\vec{r}_{\perp})
, we have

\displaystyle r^{\mu}=r_{T}\bigl(e^{-\eta_{\rm Lab}}\,,\,e^{\eta_{\rm Lab}}\,,\,\hat{e}_{x}\cos\phi+\hat{e}_{y}\sin\phi\>\bigr)_{\rm Lab}\,,

(A.39)

where
\hat{e}_{x}
and
\hat{e}_{y}
are unit vectors in the
x
and
y
directions.

The incoming lepton has no
\perp
-momentum in the lab frame but does in the Breit frame, so transforming between these frames requires both a rotation and a boost.
Here we orient our Breit frame such that the lepton
\perp
-momenta in eq. (
2.10
) are along the negative
x
-axis. Transforming the lab frame momentum in eq. (
A.38
) to the Breit frame gives

\displaystyle r^{\mu}=r_{T}\bigg(

\displaystyle\frac{e^{\eta_{\rm Lab}}}{2}\sqrt{\frac{y}{x\gamma^{2}}}+e^{-\eta_{\rm Lab}}\sqrt{\frac{x\gamma^{2}}{y}}\Big(1-\frac{y}{2}\Big)-\sqrt{1-y}\cos(\phi)\,,

\displaystyle\cos(\phi)-e^{-\eta_{\rm Lab}}\sqrt{\frac{x\gamma^{2}(1-y)}{y}}\,,\>\sin(\phi)\,,

\displaystyle\frac{e^{\eta_{\rm Lab}}}{2}\sqrt{\frac{y}{x\gamma^{2}}}-\frac{y}{2}e^{-\eta_{\rm Lab}}\sqrt{\frac{x\gamma^{2}}{y}}-\sqrt{1-y}\cos(\phi)\bigg)_{\rm Breit}\,.

(A.40)

In lightcone coordinates
(r^{+},r^{-},\vec{r}_{\perp})
, we have

\displaystyle r^{\mu}=r_{T}\Bigg(

\displaystyle e^{-\eta_{\rm Lab}}\sqrt{\frac{x\gamma^{2}}{y}}\,,\>e^{-\eta_{\rm Lab}}\sqrt{\frac{x\gamma^{2}}{y}}(1-y)+e^{\eta_{\rm Lab}}\sqrt{\frac{y}{x\gamma^{2}}}-2\sqrt{1-y}\cos(\phi)\,,

\displaystyle\hat{e}_{x}\bigg[\cos\phi-e^{-\eta_{\rm Lab}}\sqrt{\frac{x\gamma^{2}(1-y)}{y}}\>\bigg]+\hat{e}_{y}\sin\phi\Bigg)_{\rm Breit}\,.

(A.41)

Thus the relation between the Breit and lab frame rapidities is

\displaystyle e^{2\eta_{\rm Breit}}

\displaystyle=\frac{y}{x\gamma^{2}}\,\bigg(e^{2\eta_{\rm Lab}}-2\sqrt{\frac{x\gamma^{2}(1-y)}{y}}\cos(\phi)\>e^{\eta_{\rm Lab}}+\frac{x\gamma^{2}(1-y)}{y}\bigg)\,,

(A.42)

and the energy in the Breit frame is

\displaystyle E_{\rm Breit}=

\displaystyle r_{T}\bigg(\frac{e^{\eta_{\rm Lab}}}{2}\sqrt{\frac{y}{x\gamma^{2}}}+e^{-\eta_{\rm Lab}}\sqrt{\frac{x\gamma^{2}}{y}}\Big(1-\frac{y}{2}\Big)-\sqrt{1-y}\cos(\phi)\bigg)\,.

(A.43)

These expressions are functions of
\sqrt{x\gamma^{2}}=\sqrt{xE_{p}/E_{e}}
. Note that at HERA and the EIC,
\gamma\gg 1
due to
E_{p}\gg E_{e}
. In contrast, we always have
x\ll 1
in (quasi-)diffraction.

A.2
Soft function amplitude for
N
-Glauber exchange

Here, we carry out detailed calculations of the soft function in
section
6.1
, at tree level in the soft sector (
q\bar{q}
) and with an arbitrary number
N
of Glauber exchanges. For convenience we work in the Breit frame.
As we see in
figure
16
, Glauber gluons can attach to both the soft quark and soft anti-quark. We write
A_{(N_{q},N_{\bar{q}})}
to denote an amplitude involving
(N_{q},N_{\bar{q}})
Glauber exchanges attached to (
q
,
\bar{q}
), respectively. We begin by discussing the simple planar cases
A_{(N,0)}
and
A_{(0,N)}
.
Explicitly writing down a beam function can assist us in our calculations, and we can factor out the beam contributions from our final result. The beam function can be proven to not affect the value of the soft function due to the collapse rules (though it is a bit subtle how the correct combinatoric factors arise in different cases), so we choose it to be a simple fermion line.
For the planar case with all Glaubers attaching to the quark line, we have

\displaystyle A_{(N,0)}=

\displaystyle(8\pi\alpha_{s}\iota^{\epsilon}\mu^{2\epsilon})^{N}\int\prod_{i}\left(\frac{{d\!\!{}^{-}}\!\tau_{i}^{+}{d\!\!{}^{-}}\!\tau_{i}^{-}}{2}|2\tau_{i}^{z}|^{-\eta}\right)\delta(\tau^{+}-\sum_{i=1}^{N}\tau_{i}^{+})\delta(\tau^{-}-\sum_{i=1}^{N}\tau_{i}^{-})\nu^{(N-1)\eta}{}

\displaystyle\hskip-42.67912pt\times\bar{u}(k_{1})T^{A_{N}}\frac{k^{+}_{1}}{(k_{1}\!-\!\tau_{N})^{2}}\cdots T^{A_{3}}\frac{k^{+}_{1}}{(k_{1}\!-\!\sum_{i=3}^{N}\tau_{i})^{2}}T^{A_{2}}\frac{k^{+}_{1}}{(k_{1}\!-\!\sum_{i=2}^{N}\tau_{i})^{2}}\frac{\not{n}}{2}T^{A_{1}}\frac{\not{k}_{1}\!-\!\not{\tau}_{\perp}}{(k_{1}\!-\!\tau)^{2}}\gamma^{\mu}v(-k_{2}){}

\displaystyle\hskip-42.67912pt{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\times\bar{u}_{n}(p-\tau)T^{A_{N}}\frac{p^{-}}{(p-\tau+\tau_{N})^{2}}\cdots T^{A_{3}}\frac{p^{-}}{(p-\tau_{1}-\tau_{2})^{2}}T^{A_{2}}\frac{p^{-}}{(p-\tau_{1})^{2}}\frac{\not{\bar{n}}}{2}T^{A_{1}}u_{n}(p)}{}

\displaystyle\hskip-42.67912pt{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\times\int\prod_{i}\left({d\!\!{}^{-}}^{d-2}\!\tau_{i,\perp}\frac{1}{\vec{\tau}_{i\perp}^{2}}\right)\delta^{d-2}(\tau_{\perp}-\sum_{i=1}^{N}\tau_{i\perp})}\,,

(A.44)

where as before
\iota=\exp(\gamma_{E})/(4\pi)
.
Note that we drop terms that are subleading in
\lambda
from the numerators and use the projection relations

(\not{n}/2)\not{k_{i}}(\not{n}/{2})=k_{i}^{+}(\not{n}/{2})
,

(\not{n}\not{\bar{n}}/4)(\not{\bar{n}}/2)=(\not{\bar{n}}/2)
,
and

(\not{\bar{n}}\not{n}/4)(\not{n}/2)=(\not{n}/2)
. The final two lines in gray are associated with the beam function and
\perp
-convolution. Only the total Glauber momentum
\tau=\sum\tau_{i}
is constrained, so we must take
N-1
Glauber loop momentum integrals. For the
\pm
integrals, we route the Glauber loop momenta as

\displaystyle\tau_{1}=\tau+\tau_{1}^{\prime}\,,

\displaystyle\tau_{2}=\tau_{2}^{\prime}-\tau_{1}^{\prime}\,,

\displaystyle\tau_{3}=\tau_{3}^{\prime}-\tau_{2}^{\prime}\,,

\displaystyle\ldots\,,

\displaystyle\tau_{N}=-\tau_{N-1}^{\prime}\,,

(A.45)

which ensures that loop momenta do not flow through multiple propagators. This parametrization makes clear that two different types of propagators involve loop momenta; namely,

\displaystyle d_{ji}

\displaystyle\equiv(k_{j}+\tau_{i}^{\prime})^{2}=k_{j}^{+}\Big(\tau_{i}^{\prime-}-\frac{\vec{\tau}_{i\perp}^{\prime 2}+2\vec{k}_{j\perp}\cdot\vec{\tau}_{i\perp}^{\prime}}{k_{j}^{+}}+i0\Big)\equiv k_{j}^{+}\left(\tau_{i}^{\prime-}-\Delta_{ji}+i0\right)\,,

(A.46)

\displaystyle b_{i}

\displaystyle\equiv(p-\tau-\tau_{i}^{\prime})^{2}=p^{-}\Big(-\tau_{i}^{\prime+}-\tau^{+}-\frac{(\vec{\tau}_{\perp}+\vec{\tau}^{\prime}_{i\perp})^{2}}{p^{-}}+i0\Big)\equiv p^{-}\Big(-\tau_{i}^{\prime+}-\bar{\Delta}_{i}+i0\Big)\,.{}

We can then carry out the momentum integral as (see Sec.9 of ref.
[
51
]
):

\displaystyle\prod_{i=1}^{N-1}\Big(\int\frac{{d\!\!{}^{-}}\!\tau_{i}^{\prime+}{d\!\!{}^{-}}\!\tau_{i}^{\prime-}}{2}\frac{1}{d_{ji}b_{i}}\Big)|2\tau_{1}^{\prime z}|^{-\eta}|2\tau_{1}^{\prime z}-2\tau_{2}^{\prime z}|^{-\eta}\cdots|2\tau_{N-2}^{\prime z}-2\tau_{N-1}^{\prime z}|^{-\eta}|2\tau_{N-1}^{\prime z}|^{-\eta}

\displaystyle=\Big(\frac{-1}{2\,k_{1}^{+}p^{-}}\Big)^{N-1}\frac{1}{N!}\,.

(A.47)

This simplifies our amplitude to

\displaystyle A_{(N,0)}=

\displaystyle\frac{(8\pi\alpha_{s}\iota^{\epsilon}\mu^{2\epsilon})^{N}}{N!}(-1)^{N-1}\bar{u}(k_{1})\frac{\not{n}}{2}\frac{\not{k}_{1}-\not{\tau}_{\perp}}{(k_{1}-\tau)^{2}}\gamma^{\mu}T^{A_{N}}\cdots T^{A_{3}}T^{A_{2}}T^{A_{1}}v(-k_{2}){}

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\times I_{\perp}^{(N-1)}(\tau_{\perp})\times\bar{u}_{n}(p-\tau)\frac{\not{\bar{n}}}{2}T^{A_{N}}\cdots T^{A_{3}}T^{A_{2}}T^{A_{1}}u_{n}(p)\frac{1}{2^{N-1}}}\,,

(A.48)

where

\displaystyle I_{\perp}^{(N-1)}(\tau_{\perp})\equiv\int\frac{{d\!\!{}^{-}}^{d-2}\!\tau^{\prime}_{1\perp}\cdots{d\!\!{}^{-}}^{d-2}\!\tau^{\prime}_{N-1\perp}}{\left(\vec{\tau}^{\prime}_{1\perp}+\vec{\tau}_{\perp}\right)^{2}\left(\vec{\tau}^{\prime}_{2\perp}-\vec{\tau}^{\prime}_{1\perp}\right)^{2}\cdots\left(\vec{\tau}^{\prime}_{N-1\perp}-\vec{\tau}^{\prime}_{N-2\perp}\right)^{2}\vec{\tau}_{{N-1}\perp}^{2}}\,.

(A.49)

The first line of eq. (
A.2
) without the factor
(8\pi\alpha_{s}\iota^{\epsilon}\mu^{2\epsilon})^{N}/N!
is the soft function amplitude associated with
(N,0)
Glauber exchange.
24
24

24

The
(8\pi\alpha_{s}\iota^{\epsilon}\mu^{2\epsilon})^{N}
is part of the Glauber potential as discussed in eq. (
6.2
). On the other hand, the
1/N!
is part of the transverse momentum convolution between the soft and beam functions in the factorization, as shown in eq. (
4.41
), and thus is not part of the soft function amplitude.
The factor
\bar{u}_{n}\cdots u_{n}
encodes the simple fermion line beam function. Finally,
I_{\perp}^{(N-1)}(\tau_{\perp})
captures the transverse momentum exchange between the soft and beam sectors.

Likewise,
A_{(0,N)}
takes the form

\displaystyle A_{(0,N)}=

\displaystyle(8\pi\alpha_{s}\iota^{\epsilon}\mu^{2\epsilon})^{N}\int\prod_{i}\left(\frac{{d\!\!{}^{-}}\!\tau_{i}^{+}{d\!\!{}^{-}}\!\tau_{i}^{-}}{2}|2\tau_{i}^{z}|^{-\eta}\right)\delta(\tau^{+}-\sum_{i=1}^{N}\tau_{i}^{+})\delta(\tau^{-}-\sum_{i=1}^{N}\tau_{i}^{-})\nu^{(N-1)\eta}{}

\displaystyle\hskip-31.2982pt\times\bar{u}(k_{1})\gamma^{\mu}\frac{\not{\tau}{}_{\!\perp}\!-\!\not{k}_{2}}{(\tau\!-\!k_{2})^{2}}\frac{\not{n}}{2}T^{A_{1}}\frac{-k^{+}_{2}T^{A_{2}}}{(\sum_{i=2}^{N}\tau_{i}\!-\!k_{2})^{2}}\cdots\frac{-k^{+}_{2}T^{A_{N-1}}}{(\tau_{N-1}\!+\!\tau_{N}\!-\!k_{2})^{2}}\frac{-k^{+}_{2}}{(\tau_{N}\!-\!k_{2})^{2}}T^{A_{N}}v(-k_{2}){}

\displaystyle\hskip-31.2982pt{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\times\bar{u}_{n}(p-\tau)T^{A_{N}}\frac{p^{-}}{(p-\tau+\tau_{N})^{2}}\cdots\frac{\not{\bar{n}}}{2}T^{A_{3}}\frac{p^{-}\frac{\not{n}}{2}}{(p-\tau_{1}-\tau_{2})^{2}}\frac{\not{\bar{n}}}{2}T^{A_{2}}\frac{p^{-}\frac{\not{n}}{2}}{(p-\tau_{1})^{2}}\frac{\not{\bar{n}}}{2}T^{A_{1}}u_{n}(p)}{}

\displaystyle\hskip-31.2982pt{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\times\int\prod_{i}\left({d\!\!{}^{-}}^{d-2}\!\tau_{i,\perp}\frac{1}{\vec{\tau}_{i\perp}^{2}}\right)\delta^{d-2}(\tau_{\perp}-\sum_{i=1}^{N}\tau_{i\perp})}\,.

(A.50)

Carrying out Glauber collapse, we see that

\displaystyle A_{(0,N)}=

\displaystyle\frac{(8\pi\alpha_{s}\iota^{\epsilon}\mu^{2\epsilon})^{N}}{N!}\bar{u}(k_{1})\gamma^{\mu}\frac{\not{\tau}_{\perp}-\not{k}_{2}}{(\tau-k_{2})^{2}}\frac{\not{n}}{2}T^{A_{1}}T^{A_{2}}T^{A_{3}}\cdots T^{A_{N}}v(-k_{2}){}

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\times I_{\perp}^{(N-1)}(\tau_{\perp})\times\bar{u}_{n}(p-\tau)\frac{\not{\bar{n}}}{2}T^{A_{N}}\cdots T^{A_{3}}T^{A_{2}}T^{A_{1}}u_{n}(p)\frac{1}{2^{N-1}}}\,.

(A.51)

Finally, we consider the mixed case
N_{q},N_{\bar{q}}\neq 0
. Before Glauber collapse, we separate Glaubers that connect to quark and anti-quark lines, giving

\displaystyle A_{(N_{q},N_{\bar{q}})}=(8\pi\alpha_{s}\iota^{\epsilon}\mu^{2\epsilon})^{N}\!\!\!\!\sum_{\sigma=\{\sigma_{q},\sigma_{\bar{q}}\}}\int\prod_{i=1}^{N_{q}}\biggl(\frac{{d\!\!{}^{-}}\!\tau_{i,\sigma_{q}}^{+}{d\!\!{}^{-}}\!\tau_{i,\sigma_{q}}^{-}}{2}|2\tau_{i,\sigma_{q}}^{z}|^{-\eta}\biggr)\prod_{j=1}^{N_{\bar{q}}}\biggl(\frac{{d\!\!{}^{-}}\!\tau_{j,\sigma_{\bar{q}}}^{+}{d\!\!{}^{-}}\!\tau_{j,\sigma_{\bar{q}}}^{-}}{2}|2\tau_{j,\sigma_{\bar{q}}}^{z}|^{-\eta}\biggr)

\displaystyle\times\nu^{(N-1)\eta}\>\frac{{d\!\!{}^{-}}\!\tau_{\sigma_{q}}^{+}{d\!\!{}^{-}}\!\tau_{\sigma_{q}}^{-}}{2}|2\tau_{\sigma_{q}}^{z}|^{-\eta}\frac{{d\!\!{}^{-}}\!\tau_{\sigma_{\bar{q}}}^{+}{d\!\!{}^{-}}\!\tau_{\sigma_{\bar{q}}}^{-}}{2}|2\tau_{\sigma_{\bar{q}}}^{z}|^{-\eta}\delta(\tau_{\sigma_{q}}^{+}-\sum_{k=1}^{N_{q}}\tau_{k,\sigma_{q}}^{+})\delta(\tau_{\sigma_{q}}^{-}-\sum_{k=1}^{N_{q}}\tau_{k,\sigma_{q}}^{-})

\displaystyle\times\delta(\tau_{\sigma_{\bar{q}}}^{+}-\sum_{k=1}^{N_{\bar{q}}}\tau_{k,\sigma_{\bar{q}}}^{+})\delta(\tau_{\sigma_{\bar{q}}}^{-}-\sum_{k=1}^{N_{\bar{q}}}\tau_{k,\sigma_{\bar{q}}}^{-})\delta(\tau^{+}-\tau_{\sigma_{q}}^{+}-\tau_{\sigma_{\bar{q}}}^{+})\delta(\tau^{-}-\tau_{\sigma_{q}}^{-}-\tau_{\sigma_{\bar{q}}}^{-}){}

\displaystyle\times\bar{u}(k_{1})\frac{T^{A(\sigma_{q}^{N_{q}})}k^{+}_{1}}{(k_{1}-\tau_{N,\sigma_{q}})^{2}}\cdots\frac{T^{A(\sigma_{q}^{3})}k^{+}_{1}}{(k_{1}-\sum_{i=3}^{N}\tau_{i,\sigma_{q}})^{2}}\frac{T^{A(\sigma_{q}^{2})}k^{+}_{1}}{(k_{1}-\sum_{i=2}^{N}\tau_{i,\sigma_{q}})^{2}}\frac{\not{n}}{2}T^{A(\sigma_{q}^{1})}\frac{\not{k}_{1}-\not{\tau}_{q,\perp}}{(k_{1}-\tau_{\sigma_{q}})^{2}}

\displaystyle\times\gamma^{\mu}\frac{\not{\tau}_{\bar{q},\perp}-\not{k}_{2}}{(\tau_{\sigma_{\bar{q}}}-k_{2})^{2}}\frac{\not{n}}{2}T^{A(\sigma_{\bar{q}}^{1})}\frac{-k^{+}_{2}T^{A(\sigma_{\bar{q}}^{2})}}{(\sum_{i=2}^{N}\tau_{i,\sigma_{\bar{q}}}-k_{2})^{2}}\cdots\frac{-k^{+}_{2}T^{A(\sigma_{\bar{q}}^{N-1})}}{(\tau_{N-1,\sigma_{\bar{q}}}+\tau_{N,\sigma_{\bar{q}}}-k_{2})^{2}}\frac{-k^{+}_{2}T^{A(\sigma_{\bar{q}}^{N})}}{(\tau_{N,\sigma_{\bar{q}}}-k_{2})^{2}}v(-k_{2}){}

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\times\bar{u}_{n}(p-\tau)T^{A_{N}}\frac{p^{-}}{(p-\tau+\tau_{N})^{2}}\cdots T^{A_{3}}\frac{p^{-}}{(p-\tau_{1}-\tau_{2})^{2}}T^{A_{2}}\frac{p^{-}}{(p-\tau_{1})^{2}}\frac{\not{\bar{n}}}{2}T^{A_{1}}u_{n}(p)}{}

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\times\int\prod_{i}\left({d\!\!{}^{-}}^{d-2}\!\tau_{i,\perp}\frac{1}{\vec{\tau}_{i\perp}^{2}}\right)\delta^{d-2}(\tau_{\perp}-\sum_{i=1}^{N}\tau_{i\perp})}\,.

(A.52)

As discussed in
section
6.2
, we order Glauber momenta
\tau_{i}
from left to right for convenience. Additionally, we describe which Glaubers attach to the
q
and
\bar{q}
in a given permutation
\sigma
by defining sets
\sigma=\{\sigma_{q},\sigma_{\bar{q}}\}
. We can relabel momenta
\tau_{i}\to\tau_{j,\sigma_{X}}
by where they lie in the set
\sigma_{X}
. For example, if in a permutation
\sigma
we take all odd-numbered momenta to attach to the quark
X=q
, then we have
\sigma_{q}=\{1,3,...,2m-1\}
and thus

\displaystyle\{\tau_{1},\tau_{3},\cdots,\tau_{2m-1}\}\equiv

\displaystyle\{\tau_{1,\sigma_{q}},\tau_{2,\sigma_{q}},\cdots,\tau_{m,\sigma_{q}}\}\,,

(A.53)

and similarly for
\sigma_{\bar{q}}
. We also relabel
T^{A}
in this manner.

Performing the collapse in eq. (
A.2
), we have

\displaystyle A_{(N_{q},N_{\bar{q}})}=

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}I_{\perp}^{(N-1)}(\tau_{\perp})\times}\frac{(8\pi\alpha_{s}\iota^{\epsilon}\mu^{2\epsilon})^{N}}{N!}\frac{1}{k_{1}^{+}k_{2}^{+}}(-1)^{N_{q}}\sum_{\sigma}\bar{u}(k_{1})\frac{\not{n}}{2}(\not{k}_{1}-\not{\tau}_{\sigma_{q},\perp})\gamma^{\mu}(\not{\tau}_{\sigma_{\bar{q}}\perp}-\not{k}_{2})\frac{\not{n}}{2}{}

\displaystyle\times T^{A(\sigma_{q}^{N_{q}})}T^{A(\sigma_{q}^{N_{q}-1})}\cdots T^{A(\sigma_{q}^{2})}T^{A(\sigma_{q}^{1})}T^{A({\sigma_{\bar{q}}^{1}})}\,T^{A(\sigma_{\bar{q}}^{2})}\cdots T^{A(\sigma_{\bar{q}}^{\mbox{\tiny$N_{\bar{q}}-1$}})}T^{A(\sigma_{\bar{q}}^{\mbox{\tiny$N_{\bar{q}}$}})}v(-k_{2}){}

\displaystyle\times\frac{1}{\tau^{-}+\frac{1}{k_{1}^{+}}(\vec{\tau}_{\sigma_{q},\perp}-2\vec{k}_{1,\perp})\cdot\vec{\tau}_{\sigma_{q},\perp}+\frac{1}{k_{2}^{+}}(\vec{\tau}_{\sigma_{\bar{q}},\perp}-2\vec{k}_{2,\perp})\cdot\vec{\tau}_{\sigma_{\bar{q}},\perp}}{}

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\times\bar{u}_{n}(p-\tau)\frac{\not{\bar{n}}}{2}T^{A_{N}}\cdots T^{A_{3}}T^{A_{2}}T^{A_{1}}u_{n}(p)\frac{1}{2^{N-1}}}\,,

(A.54)

where
\tau_{\sigma_{X}}\equiv\sum_{i\in\sigma_{X}}\tau_{i}
. Note that
I_{\perp}^{(N-1)}(\tau_{\perp})
is a short-hand notation for the
\perp
-integration in eq. (
A.49
), which cannot be factored out completely due to the nontrivial
\perp
-dependence in the
(N_{q},N_{\bar{q}})
-soft amplitude. For this reason, we write
I_{\perp}^{(N-1)}(\tau_{\perp})
before the soft amplitude in eq. (
A.2
).
Removing the overall beam function component and the
\perp
-integrals, the
N
-Glauber soft part of the amplitude is

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}^{\mu,\{A\}}_{N\alpha\beta}=

\displaystyle\sum_{N_{q}=0}^{N}\frac{1}{k_{1}^{+}k_{2}^{+}}(-1)^{N_{q}}\sum_{\sigma}\bar{u}(k_{1})\frac{\not{n}}{2}(\not{k}_{1}-\not{\tau}_{\sigma_{q},\perp})\gamma^{\mu}(\not{\tau}_{\sigma_{\bar{q}}\perp}-\not{k}_{2})\frac{\not{n}}{2}{}

\displaystyle\times T^{A(\sigma_{q}^{N_{q}})}T^{A(\sigma_{q}^{N_{q}-1})}\cdots T^{A(\sigma_{q}^{2})}T^{A(\sigma_{q}^{1})}T^{A({\sigma_{\bar{q}}^{1}})}\,T^{A(\sigma_{\bar{q}}^{2})}\cdots T^{A(\sigma_{\bar{q}}^{\mbox{\tiny$N_{\bar{q}}-1$}})}T^{A(\sigma_{\bar{q}}^{\mbox{\tiny$N_{\bar{q}}$}})}v(-k_{2}){}

\displaystyle\times\frac{1}{\tau^{-}+\frac{1}{n\cdot k_{1}}(\vec{\tau}_{\sigma_{q},\perp}-2\vec{k}_{1,\perp})\cdot\vec{\tau}_{\sigma_{q},\perp}+\frac{1}{n\cdot k_{2}}(\vec{\tau}_{\sigma_{\bar{q}},\perp}-2\vec{k}_{2,\perp})\cdot\vec{\tau}_{\sigma_{\bar{q}},\perp}}\,.

(A.55)

Performing a color projection like eq. (
6.2
) and removing the factor
(8\pi\alpha_{s}\iota^{\epsilon}\mu^{2\epsilon})^{N}/N!
gives us the final
N
-Glauber
soft amplitude expression in eq. (
6.51
).
